   125	### 2.3 The listener
   126	
   127	**New module `src/listener/wake.ts`** (`WakeSubscriber`) built on the Realtime client of `@supabase/supabase-js`, which the CLI already depends on (`package.json`), so no second copy of `realtime-js` is introduced. `createClient(target.url, target.anonKey, { auth: { persistSession: false, autoRefreshToken: false } }).realtime`, `setAuth(anonKey)`, `heartbeatIntervalMs` at the library default 25 s. It exposes:
   128	
   129	- `state`: `disconnected | connecting | subscribed | errored(code)`; `subscribedAt`, `reconnects`, `lastWakeAt`, `lastErrorCode`, `topic` (redacted in any rendering).
   130	- `next()`: resolves once per coalesced wake and once per transition to `subscribed`. **It latches:** a wake that arrives while nobody awaits `next()` is held until the next call; the `Promise.race` below cannot drop one. **It is budgeted:** the server refuses more than `DELIVERY_CLAIM_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = 120` claims per principal per minute (`durable-delivery.ts:21`), and a refusal is not quiet — `checkDeliveryRateLimit` (`command/index.ts:4905-4934`) writes an audit row with reason `delivery_claim_rate_limited` **and** a `swarm.security_alerts` row. A wake-driven claim rate must therefore never approach it. The coalescing window is `WAKE_COALESCE_MS = 1_000` after any claim, and `WAKE_CLAIMS_PER_MINUTE_BUDGET = 50` is enforced client-side: past it the subscriber stops claiming on wakes for the rest of that minute and the reconcile catches up. The refusal fires on the 121st claim in a minute (`count > limit`, `command/index.ts:4904`), so two listeners on one credential (§9) issue at most 2 × 50 + 2 reconciles = 102, under the limit; a third would reach 153 and is the case §9 rules out. A `rate_limited` refusal, if one still arrives, flips the listener to the 15 s poll for 60 s and records `wake.rateLimited`.
   131	- `setTopic(topic)`: when a read, claim, or renewal reports a different `wake.topic` (rotation): unsubscribe old, subscribe new; the transition to `subscribed` on the new topic triggers a reconcile.
   132	
   133	The websocket bypasses `ListenerHttpClient` (`src/listener/http-client.ts`), the HTTP keep-alive adapter; that is expected and its metrics stay HTTP-only.
   134	
   135	**Loop change in `src/listener/runtime.ts`.** Two idle ticks replace one:
   136	
   137	- **Wake tick** (on `next()` resolving with a wake): claim until empty. No read.
   138	- **Reconcile tick** (every `LISTENER_RECONCILE_POLL_MS = 300_000` while `state === subscribed`; every `LISTENER_IDLE_POLL_MS` otherwise; and once on every transition to `subscribed`): **read, then claim**, exactly today's tick. The read is kept on the reconcile for three reasons, each of which a claim cannot supply: it feeds `classifyDeliveryMode` (`runtime.ts:1104`, `:612-630`), so a push-mode listener still observes the server losing the claim capability (the rollback §6 relies on); it feeds the read-health episode tracking (`:1085-1103`); and it is the call that **stamps grant use** (`record_renewal_grant_use` is on the read path, `read/index.ts:490-496`, and on the protocol path with renewals excluded, `command/index.ts:9024-9036`; claims return before it, `:8433-8452`). A standing grant self-suspends after 14 days without a use stamp (`20260901000001_standing_grants.sql:199-207`); with a reconcile read every 5 minutes an idle push-mode seat is stamped 288 times a day and never pauses itself. It costs one read per 5 minutes.
   139	
   140	The idle wait is `await Promise.race([ wake.next(), sleep(pollMsFor(wake.state)) ])`. If a wake arrives while a claim is in flight, the subscriber's latch holds it and the loop claims once more before sleeping. That is the whole duplicate-wake story: extra wakes cost one empty claim each, never a lost delivery. `cursor_fallback` mode (server without claim capability) is unchanged: the read stays on every tick and the loop behaves as today.
   141	
   142	**Status** (`src/listener/control.ts` `ListenerStatus`, `cswarm listen status`). The status parser is closed at the top level only: `STATUS_ALLOWED_KEYS` (`control.ts:225-274`) and `STATUS_SENSITIVE_KEYS` (`:282-297`) are checked at `:377-381` and `:550` over top-level keys, and the one nested block today, `readHealth`, has its own sub-parser `parseListenerReadHealth` (`:396-398`) with its own closed list (`read-health.ts:253-261`). So the `wake` block is specified the same way, or the field this spec forbids would round-trip unvalidated into `status.json`:
   143	- top-level key `wake`, admitted to `STATUS_ALLOWED_KEYS`; parsed by a new `parseListenerWake(row.wake, rejectUnknownKeys)` with the closed key list `mode`, `subscribedAt`, `reconnects`, `lastWakeAt`, `lastReconcileAt`, `errorCode`, `topicRotatedAt`, `rateLimited`; unknown keys rejected the way `readHealth` rejects them;
   144	- `topic`, `wakeTopic`, and `wake_topic` added to `STATUS_SENSITIVE_KEYS`, and the sensitive check applied inside the sub-parser too, so a topic can be neither written nor read back at any depth;
   145	- `mode` is `"push" | "poll"` and is a different word from `deliveryMode` (`STATUS_DELIVERY_MODES`, `control.ts:224`): `deliveryMode` says how work is claimed, `wake.mode` says how the listener learns there is work; `errorCode` is named so it does not collide with the top-level `lastErrorCode`.
   146	
   147	The human line reads `push (Realtime), last wake 12 s ago, reconcile every 5 min` or `poll every 15 s — Realtime not connected (<code>)`. It never says `push` unless `state === subscribed` right now (false-success rule).
   148	
   149	**Read health must not rescore the past.** `claimCadenceMs` is one scalar (`read-health.ts:40`, set wholesale by `recordListenerClaimCadence`, `:202-207`) and `expectedClaims = HOUR_MS / claimCadenceMs` is computed once from the *current* value (`:403`) and applied to every past hour of the window in the loop that follows (`:404-413`), then rendered as a throughput lapse below `LISTENER_THROUGHPUT_LAPSE_RATIO = 0.5` (`:12`, `:415`; `src/cli.ts:4664`). Push↔poll changes the cadence 12↔240 per hour, and §2.4 lists five routine reasons for it, so as coded a fallback would retroactively mark every healthy push hour a lapse. L4 therefore stores `expectedClaims` **per hour** when the hour closes, from the cadence in force for the majority of that hour, and excludes from lapse scoring any hour in which `wake.mode` changed; the tests that round-trip the cadence (`tests/listener-control.test.ts:2336`, fixture at `tests/listener-host-limits.test.ts:229`) change with it.
   150	
   151	### 2.4 Failure modes, one by one
   152	
   153	| failure | what happens | worst case | why no delivery is lost |
   154	|---|---|---|---|
   155	| **Missed wake** (Realtime dropped it; broadcast has no replay) | nothing until the reconcile | 5 min latency | the row is in `signal_deliveries`; the reconcile claim reads the ledger |
   156	| **Socket down, listener does not know yet** (half-open) | library heartbeat every 25 s detects it; state → `disconnected`; poll at 15 s begins | 25 s detection + 15 s poll = 40 s | same |
   157	| **Socket down, known** | 15 s poll; reconnect ladder `[1, 2, 5, 10] s` (`RealtimeClient.js:16`) | 15 s latency | same |
   158	| **Reconnect** | transition to `subscribed` fires one reconcile (reconcile-on-join) | — | any row enqueued while disconnected is claimed on join; a wake in the window between server-side join acceptance and the client's `SUBSCRIBED` callback is delivered, because bindings are registered before `subscribe()` and inbound dispatch gates only on channel-control events |
   159	| **Duplicate wake** (two listeners on one principal, coalescing miss) | each subscriber claims; `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`durable-delivery.ts:293`) gives the row to one | one extra empty claim per duplicate | leases are exclusive |
   160	| **Wake id rotated** (a token of this principal was revoked) | the old topic goes silent; the next read/claim/renewal carries the new topic; `setTopic` resubscribes; reconcile on `subscribed` | one reconcile interval, or 15 s if the socket was down | the reconcile |
   161	| **Policy refused** (principal revoked, or a stale id after rotation) | `CHANNEL_ERROR`; state → `errored(unauthorized)`; poll at 15 s; the next read carries the current topic if the principal is live | 15 s latency until re-keyed | claim still works if the token is live; if it is not, the seat is dead by design |
   162	| **Reconnect storm** (Realtime deploy, network blip, our own release) | every seat reconnects on the library ladder; joins are rate-limited server-side (500/s on Pro, `too_many_joins`); the client adds 0–5 s jitter before the reconcile | at 10,000 seats ≈ 20 s of throttled joins, then normal | the 15 s poll covers the window |
   163	| **Thundering herd on our deploy** (all listeners restart on a release) | each start = 1 join + 1 reconcile (read + claim) | 10,000 seats → ≈ 30,000 invocations in one minute, once | — |
   164	| **Wake for a row already claimed** (reconcile raced the wake) | one empty claim | — | — |
   165	| **Realtime message rate exceeded** (500/s Pro) | Realtime disconnects; the client reconnects when under the limit | poll covers | — |
   166	| **Claim rate limit reached** (120/min per principal, `durable-delivery.ts:21`; the 121st claim is refused and writes an audit row and a `security_alerts` row, `command/index.ts:4904-4933`) | the client budget (§2.3: 1 s coalescing, 50 wake-claims/min) keeps one listener at ≤ 51/min and two at ≤ 102; on a refusal the listener polls at 15 s for 60 s and records `wake.rateLimited` | 60 s of 15 s latency; one alert row | the reconcile |
   167	| **Trigger cannot send** (Realtime schema unavailable, partition missing, grant missing) | exception swallowed; row inserted; wake lost | 5 min | W4's guard; the reconcile |
   168	| **Listener behind a proxy that blocks websockets** | `connecting` never reaches `subscribed`; poll at 15 s; status says so | permanent 15 s latency | lane A's world, unchanged |
   169	
   170	### 2.5 What stays exactly as it is
   171	
   172	The claim (`claimAgentInbox`, `durable-delivery.ts:172`), the lease budget, `DELIVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, the ack path, prepared-ack recovery (`runtime.ts:1258`), receipts, the activity heartbeat transport, the hook, `--route`, `--permissions`, credential rotation. A 0.1.56 listener keeps working at every step (§6).

   176	## 3. Persistence and retention
   177	
   178	### 3.1 What a poll may persist

codex
Reviewed `22e2bea03fdeb3b48c35bab3ef643437dcedcefc`, after reading the three `74fdc45` reports. Three new DEFECTs remain.

1. **DEFECT — The wait loop does not enforce the fallback or reconcile deadlines.**  
   Spec lines 130–140 make `next()` resolve on a wake or successful subscription only. If the socket fails just after `sleep(300_000)` starts, neither event occurs. Changing `wake.state` cannot shorten that existing timer. The listener can wait nearly five minutes while status says “poll every 15 s.”

   Also, each wake starts a fresh five-minute sleep. Wakes every four minutes can prevent reconcile reads indefinitely. Those reads perform mode classification in `src/listener/runtime.ts:1075–1104`; the existing idle sleep is at `:1455–1456`.

   **Fix:** keep an absolute reconcile deadline across wakes, and interrupt the wait on connection failure. Specify cancellation of losing waiters. Test both a disconnect during sleep and repeated wakes across the reconcile deadline.

2. **DEFECT — The 25-second failure-detection bound is false.**  
   Spec line 156 and §8 promise detection within 25 seconds. The installed Phoenix code waits one heartbeat interval after a reply before sending again (`node_modules/@supabase/phoenix/assets/js/phoenix/socket.js:709–712`), then another interval before declaring timeout (`:677–685`). Timeout raises the channel error at `:473–485`.

   A connection that fails just after a heartbeat reply can therefore take nearly **50 seconds** to detect, before fallback polling begins. Even with the wait-loop fix, the stated 40-second total becomes approximately 65 seconds, excluding scheduling and request time.

   **Fix:** correct the failure table and measurement targets. Test a silent network failure immediately after a heartbeat reply.

3. **DEFECT — Capacity omits the existing activity broadcasts.**  
   §7 counts W1 and W2 messages but retains 1,440 activity calls per seat-day. Each accepted activity call also executes `realtime.send` (`supabase/functions/activity/index.ts:145–152`). Each open workspace tab subscribes to those broadcasts (`site/src/lib/commonswarm.ts:187–195`). These are application broadcasts, separate from WebSocket protocol heartbeats.

   With two open tabs per workspace, omitted activity deliveries alone are:

   - 100 seats: **288,000/day**
   - 1,000 seats: **2.88 million/day**
   - 10,000 seats: **28.8 million/day**

   That exceeds the table’s entire message estimate at each size. Ignoring fewer than ten tabs is not valid for message volume.

   **Fix:** state the tab-count assumption and include activity fan-out in message capacity and cost arithmetic.

No live network-failure test or production measurement was performed. These findings follow from the specified loop and checked source.

QUOTE-BACK: # Push delivery: wake listeners from Supabase Realtime instead of polling
VERDICT: FAIL
REASON: The wait contract fails its stated deadlines, heartbeat detection takes longer than claimed, and capacity omits activity broadcasts.

### 2. Inversion Arguments Evaluation

#### Inversion (a): That owning the trigger functions as `postgres` is unsafe or unavailable on hosted Supabase.
- **Inversion Claim:** Assigning trigger functions to `postgres` represents an unacceptable privilege escalation vulnerability or will be rejected/unavailable in managed Supabase environments.
- **Spec & Evidence Analysis:**
  1. *Availability:* All migrations run through the Supabase CLI (`supabase db push`) or dashboard connect as the administrative superuser role `postgres`. `ALTER FUNCTION ... OWNER TO postgres` is standard across Supabase extensions and migrations. Furthermore, `realtime.send` is deployed into schema `realtime`, where `postgres` natively holds `USAGE` and `INSERT` privileges on `realtime.messages`.
  2. *Necessity:* Opus D1 established on the local stack that `swarm_admin` lacks `USAGE` on `realtime` and `INSERT` on `realtime.messages` (`has_schema_privilege('swarm_admin','realtime','USAGE') = false`). Granting table-level permissions to `swarm_admin` fails because `realtime.messages` is partitioned daily by the Realtime service, and newly created partitions do not reliably inherit grants.
  3. *Safety & Containment:* The trigger functions (`swarm.wake_agent_delivery()`, `swarm.wake_workspace_signal()`) are hardened with `SET search_path = pg_catalog`, preventing search-path hijacking. All table accesses (`swarm.agent_principals`) and function calls (`realtime.send`) are fully schema-qualified. The trigger takes no user parameters, executes no dynamic SQL, and only emits a content-free broadcast (`signal_id`, `enqueued_at`).
  4. *Codebase Precedent:* The existing activity edge function already relies on `RESET ROLE` to revert to the connection role `postgres` precisely to invoke `realtime.send` (`activity/index.ts:139-145`).
- **Verdict:** **BEATEN BY SPEC (Refuted).**

---

#### Inversion (b): That re-creating `agent_delivery_read_context` with an extra output column breaks a 0.1.56 client or the read edge during the apply window.
- **Inversion Claim:** Replacing `swarm.agent_delivery_read_context` via `DROP FUNCTION` / `CREATE FUNCTION` to add the 11th `OUT` column (`wake_id`) breaks backward compatibility for 0.1.56 listeners and causes runtime failures in `read/index.ts` during migration deployment.
- **Spec & Evidence Analysis:**
  1. *0.1.56 Client Isolation:* 0.1.56 listeners invoke the HTTP endpoint `POST /functions/v1/read`, never database functions directly. The response parser in `src/cloud/signals.ts:1211-1218` strictly parses known fields and silently drops unrecognized keys. The addition of `wake: { topic, event }` in the response is purely additive.
  2. *Apply Window Invariant:* In Postgres, function replacement inside a DDL transaction is atomic. The input argument signature `(bytea, uuid)` remains identical. Adding an OUT parameter changes the composite record return type; calling queries in the pre-L3 `read/index.ts:437-451` that select explicit columns continue resolving their requested fields without error.
  3. *Apply Sequence:* The lane sequencing in §6 explicitly enforces: L2 migration applied first → L3 read/command edge deployed second → L4 client deployed last. Pre-L3 `read` ignores the 11th column until L3 deploys; 0.1.56 clients ignore `wake` indefinitely and continue polling at their existing cadence.
- **Verdict:** **BEATEN BY SPEC (Refuted).**

---

#### Inversion (c): That the lane order still lets two lanes hold the local database at once.
- **Inversion Claim:** The revised lane schedule in §6 still allows concurrent database test execution or lock contention across lanes sharing the local database container.
- **Spec & Evidence Analysis:**
  1. *Resource Serialization Rule:* The local database is explicitly treated as an exclusive lock resource: any lane executing `test:p1-local` or `test:p1-server` requires exclusive access to prevent `db:reset` collisions (`tests/p1-cli/test-gate-coverage.test.ts:19-21`).
  2. *Verification of Database-Holding Lanes:*
     - L0 (idle cost constants): touches no database.
     - L1 (claim persistence): runs `test:p1-server`. Holds local DB exclusively.
     - L2c (credential redaction): pure unit tests, touches no database. Runs in parallel with L0/L1.
     - L2 (wake migration): runs `test:p1-local`. Scheduled strictly *after* L1 merges.
     - L2b (retention policy): runs `test:p1-local`. Scheduled strictly *after* L2 merges.
     - L3 (wake response & rotation): runs `test:p1-server`. Scheduled strictly *after* L2 merges.
     - L4 (listener client): fake-socket tests in CI; live local validation is an isolated manual step. Scheduled strictly *after* L0, L2b, L2c, and L3.
     - L5, L6, L7, L8: sequenced downstream; L6 runs site tests only with zero database interaction.
  3. All lanes touching the database (`L1 -> L2 -> L2b -> L3`) form a strictly serialized chain.
- **Verdict:** **BEATEN BY SPEC (Refuted).**

---

#### Inversion (d): That 'revocation by intent' leaves a credential path whose stale subscriber learns more than 'principal X has work'.
- **Inversion Claim:** Because rotation does not trigger on database-level cascades (such as grant expiry, run completion, or workspace archival), a stale subscriber holding an unrotated `wake_id` can observe sensitive message contents or metadata.
- **Spec & Evidence Analysis:**
  1. *Payload Boundary:* W1 and W4 define the wake payload strictly as:
     `{ "v": 1, "signal_id": "<uuid>", "enqueued_at": "<timestamptz>" }`
     The payload carries zero signal bodies, no sender principal, no channel names, no kinds, and no arguments.
  2. *Claim & Read Enforcement:* A wake conveys no capability to read data. To claim or read the payload, an agent must present a valid, unrevoked bearer token (`swm_agt_...`) to `POST /functions/v1/command` or `/read`. When credentials expire or are revoked via cascade, the claim/read calls are rejected with 401/403.
  3. *Channel Authorization:* Realtime RLS policy `agent receives its own wake` (W5) restricts anon listeners to matching `cswarm-wake:{wake_id}`. Stale subscribers cannot join workspace topics (`cswarm-signals:{workspace_id}`) or other principals' topics. The maximum possible leakage for a stale subscriber is knowing that a signal was enqueued for principal X at timestamp T—exactly "principal X has work".
- **Verdict:** **BEATEN BY SPEC (Refuted).**

---

#### Inversion (e): That the whole design is not worth it against lane A's 15-second poll (the strongest case for doing nothing).
- **Inversion Claim:** Lane A's simple constant change to a 15-second poll reduces invocations sufficiently that building push delivery adds unnecessary complexity.
- **Spec & Evidence Analysis:**
  1. *Latency Penalties:* Lane A introduces an unavoidable delivery latency of up to 15 seconds (mean 7.5 s) on every single delivery turn. In multi-agent collaborative workflows and interactive CLI commands, adding 7.5–15 seconds per hop degrades system performance and violates the operator requirement that the system "works well". Push delivery delivers wakes in < 0.5 s (sub-second observed locally).
  2. *Scaling Asymptotics:*
     - Lane A's 15 s polling cuts invocations by ~7×, but cost continues to scale linearly with seats: `seats × (1 / cadence)`. At 1,000 seats, Lane A consumes ~7.2 million edge invocations/day (~$140/mo); at 10,000 seats, it consumes ~72 million invocations/day (~2.16 billion/month, ~$4,300/mo).
     - Push delivery reduces idle edge invocations by 96% (from ~53,800 to ~2,200 per seat/day). At 1,000 seats, edge invocations drop to ~2.4M/day; with presence (Successor 1), 10,000 seats drop to ~4.6M/day (~$280/mo).
  3. *Persistence Write Amplification:* Under Lane A polling, each seat continues executing claims that hit `checkDeliveryRateLimit` minute buckets and consume database CPU. Push delivery eliminates per-tick polling, reducing rows written per idle day from 49,400 to ~400 short-lived rate-bucket rows.
  4. *Complementarity:* Lane A is not an alternative to push delivery; it is lane L0, which serves as the safety net and fallback transport during reconnects, outages, and proxy restrictions.
- **Verdict:** **BEATEN BY SPEC (Refuted).**

---

### 3. Review Against Requirements

1. **Wire contract & failure modes:** Push-as-hint with private topic `cswarm-wake:{wake_id}`, exact JSON payload, authorization via `wake_topic_authorized`, and full breakdown of all 14 failure modes (§2.2, §2.4).
2. **Cost per idle seat per day & arithmetic:** Derived in §1.3 and §7; drops from 53,800 to 2,200 edge invocations/day (−96%) and 49,400 rows to 400 rows/day.
3. **Persistence and retention:** Zero audit/idempotency persistence on empty claims (§3.1); idempotency retention floor lowered to 1 day with 2-day active retention in L2b (§3.2).
4. **Watcher and web app:** `inbox --notify` unified on W1 (§4.1); app live feed unified on W2 workspace topic (§4.3).
5. **Indexes & query plans:** Confirmed against `local-plans.out` with partial index scan on `signal_deliveries_unacked_oldest` and unique index on `agent_principals_wake_id` (§5).
6. **Lane apply order & file ownership:** Explicit ownership matrix across L0–L8 with disjoint file assignments, test pins, and exclusive single-file local database sequencing (§6).
7. **Capacity scaling:** Evaluated at 100, 1,000, and 10,000 seats with explicit connection and message limits (§7).

**DEFECTS:** 0  
**GAPS:** 0  
**NITS:** 0

PART 1 — DRAFT-2 ITEMS: VERIFIED FIXED
=========================================================================

Section 11 claims each of my draft-2 items landed. Each is confirmed against
the code, not against the spec's prose.

DA (rotation had no home) — FIXED. There is still no swarm.revoke_agent_token;
revocation is TypeScript. W6 now names swarm.rotate_wake_id() as an L2-defined
SECURITY DEFINER function called by L3 from the revocation-by-intent sites, and
uses no AFTER UPDATE OF revoked_at trigger. discardStrandedSuccessor really is
command/index.ts:3565-3587 with the UPDATE at :3576, called from the ordinary
renewal handover inside fenceRenewal's savepoint at :3658-3667. Excluding it by
name is correct. Callability: swarm_command holds SELECT and INSERT,UPDATE on
swarm.agent_principals (20260723000001:717-762, table-level, so a new column is
covered), measured has_table_privilege('swarm_command','swarm.agent_principals',
'UPDATE') = true. The SECURITY DEFINER wrapper is not strictly required for the
UPDATE but is safe, and the existing precedent (GRANT EXECUTE ON swarm.is_member
TO swarm_command, :614) is the right template. Callable after SET LOCAL ROLE
swarm_command (command/index.ts:1377): yes. See N3/N4 for what the surrounding
sentence still gets wrong.

DB (pgcrypto / search_path) — FIXED, and re-measured. pgcrypto is installed in
schema `extensions`. Positive control on the same session:
    SET search_path = swarm, pg_catalog; SELECT gen_random_bytes(32);
    -> ERROR: function gen_random_bytes(integer) does not exist
    SELECT translate(encode(extensions.gen_random_bytes(32),'base64'),'+/=','-_');
    -> AIkz0VtVe8FmAqdCRzfObz1GFZ0_MdaXUsMDaRA-msQ   (length 43)
    5 rows from generate_series(1,5) -> 5 distinct values
So W1's 43-character claim, the base64url translate (the shorter `to` argument
deletes the '=' pad), and the volatile per-row default all hold, and the
schema-qualified form is the only one that resolves. CREATE EXTENSION ... WITH
SCHEMA extensions in L2 is the right belt.

DC (topic redaction) — FIXED. CREDENTIAL_PREFIX_RE is src/host/credential-
redaction.ts:19-22 and matches only swm_(agt|inv|cap)_, so a bare topic would
pass through today. The module is imported by supabase/functions/activity/
index.ts:2 and that file is inside `npm run check:edge` (package.json:22 names
command, read, capability AND activity), so L2c does run both gates as claimed.
The proposed alternation `cswarm-wake:[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}` is well-formed against
the existing `gi` regex and runs after the control/separator strip, so a laced
topic is reassembled before the match, same as a credential.

DD (L2/L2b contention) — FIXED for that pair. L2b is sequenced after L2, owns
the config row, and the local database is declared a file. But the same rule is
violated by a different pair — see D3.

GA (status parser closed at the top level only) — FIXED and buildable.
STATUS_ALLOWED_KEYS is control.ts:225-273, STATUS_SENSITIVE_KEYS is :282-297,
both applied over top-level keys only at :377-381, and the allowed-key filter is
re-applied at :550. readHealth is the one nested block and has its own
sub-parser at :396-398. parseListenerReadHealth (read-health.ts:246) has the
signature (value: unknown, rejectUnknownKeys = false) and rejects with
hasExpectedKeys(row, [...9 keys...], rejectUnknownKeys), returning null rather
than throwing. parseListenerWake can be built from exactly that pattern; the
spec's eight-key closed list and the "sensitive check inside the sub-parser too"
requirement both fit it, and control.ts is the file L4 already owns.

GB (read health rescoring the past) — FIXED and correctly diagnosed.
claimCadenceMs is one scalar (read-health.ts:40, set wholesale by
recordListenerClaimCadence at :202-207); `const expectedClaims = HOUR_MS /
health.claimCadenceMs` is computed once at :403 OUTSIDE the loop at :404-413 and
applied to every past hour; the lapse filter is :414-416 against
LISTENER_THROUGHPUT_LAPSE_RATIO (:12). A push->poll flip would therefore
retroactively re-score every closed hour, exactly as stated. Storing
expectedClaims per hour and excluding mode-change hours is the right fix and L4
owns the file.

GC (claim rate limit) — FIXED in substance. DELIVERY_CLAIM_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE
= 120 is durable-delivery.ts:21; checkDeliveryRateLimit is command/index.ts:4868
with the per-minute bucket at :4880-4888 and the refusal block at :4904-4933,
which writes an audit row (reason delivery_claim_rate_limited) AND a
swarm.security_alerts row, once, at count === limit + 1. The client budget is
now specified. The arithmetic around it is wrong in one sentence — see N1.

pointer. See D4.

=========================================================================
PART 2 — DEFECTS
=========================================================================

-------------------------------------------------------------------------
D1. §2.2 W4 — the wake trigger as specified CANNOT SEND, and its own
    exception guard hides that forever.
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
CLAIM. "swarm.wake_agent_delivery() AFTER INSERT ON swarm.signal_deliveries ...
calls realtime.send(payload,'wake','cswarm-wake:'||wake_id,true). SECURITY
DEFINER, owner swarm_admin, SET search_path = pg_catalog, because swarm_command
cannot execute realtime.send (the activity function has to RESET ROLE for the
same reason, activity/index.ts:139-145). The body is wrapped in BEGIN ...
EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN RETURN NULL; END so a Realtime failure can never fail
the signal insert." The same construction is specified for
swarm.wake_workspace_signal() (W2/W4). §9 adds: "the realtime.send grant to
swarm_admin [is] not in the repo (locally EXECUTE is PUBLIC, local-plans.out)".

WHAT IS WRONG. swarm_admin cannot reach schema realtime at all.

  select has_schema_privilege('swarm_admin','realtime','USAGE');        -> false
  select has_table_privilege('swarm_admin','realtime.messages','INSERT');-> false
  select has_function_privilege('swarm_admin',
         'realtime.send(jsonb,text,text,boolean)','EXECUTE');            -> true
  select rolsuper, rolbypassrls from pg_roles where rolname='swarm_admin';
                                                        -> false, false

Live probe, with a positive control on the same invocation. Two byte-identical
plpgsql functions, both SECURITY DEFINER with SET search_path = pg_catalog, both
doing PERFORM realtime.send('{"v":1}'::jsonb,'wake',t,true); the first created
in schema swarm and ALTERed to OWNER swarm_admin (verified:
"owner=swarm_admin secdef=true"), the second left owned by postgres. Both
GRANTed EXECUTE to swarm_command and both called inside a transaction after
SET LOCAL ROLE swarm_command, which is exactly what command/index.ts:1377 does:

  SELECT swarm.zz_wake_probe_admin('cswarm-wake:PROBE-ADMIN');
    ERROR:  permission denied for schema realtime
    LINE 1: SELECT realtime.send('{"v":1}'::jsonb, 'wake', t, true)
    CONTEXT: PL/pgSQL function swarm.zz_wake_probe_admin(text) line 3 at PERFORM

  positive control, same session, prior run:
  SELECT zz_wake_probe.as_postgres('cswarm-wake:PROBE-POSTGRES');
    -> topic cswarm-wake:PROBE-POSTGRES | count 1  in realtime.messages

Both objects dropped afterwards; leftover_funcs=0, leftover_schema=0,
leftover_msgs=0.

CONSEQUENCE. The failure is inside the trigger body, which W4 deliberately wraps
in EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN RETURN NULL. So: the delivery row commits, the
listener's join succeeds (W5 is unaffected), `cswarm listen status` reads
mode: "push" because state === subscribed is genuinely true, and NO WAKE IS EVER
SENT. Every seat runs on the 5-minute reconcile with nothing anywhere reporting
a fault. That is the precise false-success shape §2.3's own status rule and the
brain topic false-success-signals exist to stop, and §2.4's "Trigger cannot send"
row assumes this is a rare degradation rather than the default state.

The stated REASON is also wrong, in a way that matters for the fix.
realtime.send is SECURITY INVOKER (prosecdef=false) with proacl
`=X/supabase_admin,...`, i.e. EXECUTE to PUBLIC — swarm_command CAN execute it.
What swarm_command lacks is USAGE on schema realtime (false) and INSERT on
realtime.messages (false). activity/index.ts:139-145 works because RESET ROLE
restores the CONNECTION role (postgres), not because it becomes swarm_admin.
And realtime.send swallows its own INSERT failure with RAISE WARNING (read its
pg_get_functiondef), so even a privilege that only blocks the INSERT produces a
silent no-op rather than an error.

WHAT TO CHANGE. Either (a) own both trigger functions with the role the
connection already uses — postgres/supabase_admin — which is what the positive
control proves works and what the activity precedent actually does; or (b) have
L2's migration add GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA realtime TO swarm_admin and GRANT
INSERT ON realtime.messages TO swarm_admin (and confirm the day partition
inherits it). Either way §9's "not in the repo ... locally EXECUTE is PUBLIC"
must be rewritten: EXECUTE is not the binding privilege, and the local state is
NOT already sufficient. L2's listed test "trigger inserts one realtime.messages
row per delivery" is the right control and would catch this — but only once the
owner or the grants are fixed, and it must assert the ROW, never the absence of
an error, because the guard guarantees there is no error.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------
D2. §2.2 W3.1 / §4.1 / §6 — no lane owns the database change the read edge
    needs before it can return `wake`, so the watcher can never learn a topic.
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
CLAIM. W3.1: "The read edge's inbox page (read/index.ts:826-832 ...) gains an
optional wake: { topic, event } when the caller is an agent and inbox: true.
This is how a listener learns its topic at start ... and how the watcher learns
it without ever calling a command." §4.1 repeats it as the watcher's only
source. §6 gives L3 `supabase/functions/read/index.ts` and gives L2 an
enumerated migration containing the column, the unique index,
wake_topic_authorized, rotate_wake_id, both triggers, both policies, the comment
amendment and the test-gate lines — and no read-path grant or function change.

WHAT IS WRONG. The read edge runs as swarm_read, which cannot see the column.

  read/index.ts:417   await tx.unsafe("SET LOCAL ROLE swarm_read");
  read/index.ts:418   SET LOCAL search_path = swarm_read, swarm, pg_catalog
  select has_table_privilege('swarm_read','swarm.agent_principals','SELECT');
                                                                   -> false

swarm_read's only reach into principals is EXECUTE on the narrow SECURITY
DEFINER swarm.agent_delivery_read_context(bytea,uuid) (20260731000001:487,
re-granted 20260820000002:306), whose live signature is a fixed list:
  RETURNS TABLE(token_id, principal_id, owner_user_id, principal_workspace_id,
                run_id, device_id, first_use, membership_revoked_at,
                is_revoked, pending_delivery_count)
and read/index.ts:437-451 selects exactly those ten. There is no wake column and
no other principal read on the agent branch.

The obvious escape hatch is closed too. The view swarm_read.agent_principals
exists, but its definition is
  SELECT principal_id, workspace_id, owner_user_id, name, created_at,
         revoked_at, model FROM swarm.agent_principals p
  WHERE swarm.is_member(workspace_id, auth.uid());
and the agent branch of read/index.ts installs no request.jwt.claims (only the
human branch does, :421-428), so auth.uid() is null and the view returns no
rows for an agent.

CONSEQUENCE. W3.1 requires a migration: either a new grant, or a new column on
the swarm_read view, or an extra output column on agent_delivery_read_context —
and that last one cannot be done with CREATE OR REPLACE, because the OUT
parameter list changes, so it is a DROP/CREATE plus a re-GRANT. All three are
L2's file. L2's enumerated contents contain none of them and L3 owns no
migration. So L3 cannot build the read half of the wire contract.

This is not cosmetic. W3.2's command-side path is fine (swarm_command does hold
SELECT on agent_principals, measured true), so a LISTENER could eventually learn
its topic from a claim. The watcher cannot: arrival-watch.ts:336-338 states and
enforces the read-only invariant ("It never imports or calls delivery claim/ack
code"), and §4.1 leans on W3.1 by name. With W3.1 unbuildable, L5 has no topic
source at all and requirement (4) of the ask — the watcher on the same model —
cannot be delivered in the order §6 gives.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------
D3. §6 — L1 and L2 are scheduled in parallel and both need the exclusive
    local database, breaking the section's own rule.
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
CLAIM. "The local database is a file for this purpose: test:p1-local and
test:p1-server need the one local stack and an exclusive slot (AGENTS.md;
tests/p1-cli/test-gate-coverage.test.ts:19-21), so no two lanes that run them
are ever 'in parallel'." Then: "Order: L0, L1, L2, L2c now, in parallel
(disjoint files; only L2 uses the local database)."

WHAT IS WRONG. L1's own named test is
tests/p1-server/claim-empty-persists-nothing.test.ts. That file is reached only
by `test:p1-server` (package.json:29, glob tests/p1-server/**/*.test.ts), which
AGENTS.md's command table records as "needs local Supabase and an exclusive DB
slot", and which carries --test-concurrency=1 and a pretest hook that
regenerates supabase/functions/_shared/protocol.js. So L1 uses the local
database, "only L2 uses the local database" is false, and the parenthetical
that authorises the parallel start contradicts the rule three sentences above
it. L1 and L2 must be sequenced (either order), or L1 must run against a
separate stack that the spec does not provide.

L3 is already correctly sequenced after L1 and L2, and its tests are p1-server
too, so the rest of the order holds once this pair is fixed.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------
D4. §6 — the package.json gate lines are mis-numbered, and `npm test` is not
    pinned by anything.
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
CLAIM. "`npm test` (package.json:25) and test:p1-local (:26) are literal lists
pinned by test-gate-coverage.test.ts:23-34, :85-95, :125-133; a lane that adds a
test file owns the matching package.json line and the pin for as long as it
runs." L4: "new tests/listener-wake.test.ts and therefore package.json:25 plus
the pin, after L2b has merged". L3: "tests/p1-server/wake-topic-in-responses.
test.ts (named in package.json:27's glob, so no gate edit)".

WHAT IS WRONG. Measured at 74fdc45 and, identically, at d500973 (the SHA the
document says every citation was confirmed on):
  package.json:23  "test"           <- the literal list
  package.json:25  "test:p1-cli"    <- a GLOB, tests/p1-cli/**/*.test.ts
  package.json:26  "test:p1-local"  <- literal list, correct
  package.json:27  "test:uxtest"
  package.json:29  "test:p1-server" <- the glob L3's test is reached by
And in tests/p1-cli/test-gate-coverage.test.ts: :23-34 is localStackCommand plus
localStackTests (correct); :86-96 is the D-030 reachability test (the spec says
:85-95); :124 pins test:p1-cli's exact string; :125 pins test:p1-local's. There
is NO assertion anywhere pinning the `test` script's string.

CONSEQUENCE. L4 is told to add a new root-level test file to package.json:25.
That line is the p1-cli glob: its pattern cannot match tests/listener-wake.
test.ts, so the file stays unreachable and the D-030 reachability assertion at
:86-96 fails; and editing that line's string breaks the exact-string pin at
:124. The correct edit is package.json:23, and there is no pin for L4 to own —
which also means the stated reason for sequencing L4 after L2b ("same gate
lines") does not exist. L2/L2b are the only lanes that really own a pin, and
L3's :27 should read :29.

PART 4 — NITS
=========================================================================

N1. §2.3 / §2.4 / §9 — the two-listener claim-rate sentence contradicts the
budget in the same paragraph. checkDeliveryRateLimit refuses at
`if (count > limit)` (command/index.ts:4904), so the 121st claim in a minute is
the first refusal and the one that writes the audit + security_alerts pair
(count === limit + 1, :4905-4932). §2.3 says coalescing gives "at most 60
wake-driven claims per minute plus its reconcile", so two listeners are 122, not
"120, the limit" — they are over it, and the sentence's own conclusion is wrong
by its own terms. The number that makes the design safe is the client budget the
same paragraph specifies: WAKE_CLAIMS_PER_MINUTE_BUDGET = 50 gives 2x50 + 2 =
102, comfortably under 120, and it is what makes §9's "two is the most the
120/min claim limit tolerates at the client budget" true (three would be 153).
Keep the 50-budget arithmetic; delete the 60/120 sentence.

N2. §2.2 W1 — "the one site is command/index.ts:4025-4036" is a typed
enumeration with a second member. src/cloud/seed.ts:157 (seedDogfood, reached
from src/cli.ts:101 and from tests/p1-local/local-integration.test.ts and
tests/p1-local/human-seen-browser.test.ts) also does INSERT INTO
swarm.agent_principals, naming four columns. The volatile default still applies
and nothing breaks; the count is wrong, and this is the shape of error AGENTS.md
names ("an enumeration inside a message must be generated, not typed").

N3. §2.2 W6 — the nine-statement enumeration is right about the line numbers and
wrong about their shape. Eight of the nine (:3973, :4012, :4112, :4140, :4172,
:4181, :4208, :4237) are all inside ONE function, updateWorkspaceProjection
(:3913), i.e. all already on the event-apply path; only :3576
(discardStrandedSuccessor, :3565) sits outside it, so "(plus :4428 on an
event-apply path)" implies a distinction that does not exist. Worse, :4428 is
inside applyEventSideEffects (:4403) and is `UPDATE swarm.grants SET revoked_at`
on TaskReopened — a task grant, not a credential; it is neither agent_tokens nor
agent_principals and should not be on a list the sentence scopes to those two
tables. By that scoping only :4112 (AgentPrincipalRevoked -> agent_principals),
:4140 (its token cascade) and :4208 (AgentTokenRevoked) qualify, alongside
:3576; :3973 is swarm.invitations and :4012 is swarm.memberships. The claim that
:3576 is the only renewal-path one HOLDS: I found no other revoked_at write
between fenceRenewal (:3624) and appendEvents (:3749), and the handover paths
(20260820000002:156-173) write first_used_at and expires_at, not revoked_at.

N4. §0 / §2.2 W6 — "the id changes the moment any credential of that principal
is revoked" is stronger than what §2.2 builds. swarm.renewal_grants_revoke_
cascade() (20260728000002:413-431, trigger :441-453) stamps agent_tokens.
revoked_at from inside the database on every grant revoke, and device revoke
(command/index.ts:4534), run end, membership revoke and workspace archive each
kill a credential without touching agent_tokens.revoked_at at all. None rotate
wake_id, and W5's wake_topic_authorized checks only "a live token" via
agent_tokens_by_principal, not the fuller liveness agent_delivery_read_context
enforces. The blast radius stays exactly what W1 already concedes — a holder of
a stale id learns THAT the principal has work — so this is a wording defect, not
a hole. Say "on a revocation by intent", which is what the design actually does,
and note that the grant cascade means "revocation is not a database function" is
only true of the entry point, not of the effect.

N5. §2.2 W4 — the trigger is specified SET search_path = pg_catalog and then
described as reading "agent_principals.wake_id". Under that search_path the
reference must be written swarm.agent_principals. The two enqueue triggers the
spec cites as its model (20260905000020:152-176 and :189-217) are both
SET search_path = pg_catalog and qualify every object; L2 should follow them.

N6. Citation drift, each opened and checked: STATUS_SENSITIVE_KEYS is
control.ts:282-297, not :284-299. parseListenerReadHealth's closed key list is
